# Definitions of Done

> **TL;DR:** Setup is complete only when all three upstream installs coexist, their versions are evidenced and the Design Library docs can be read. Future component quality remains unproven until its own evidence exists.

## Owner instructions

**The single home for every owner instruction** (owner, 2026-11-07 20:13: *"Define a DoD. Every time I give instructions, it should go in the DoD immediately."*). Each instruction becomes a row here in the same turn it arrives, before any build, and every packet is checked against these rows. Quotes are verbatim (the owner's spelling), times +05:30, mined from both main-chat logs on 2026-11-07. **Done** means delivered and awaiting the owner's judgement; nothing is approved unless the [ledger](work-plan.md#approval-ledger) says so. Workflow rules (roles, cadence, delegation) live in AGENTS.md and the work plan.

**Open gaps (2026-11-07 20:42):** Q24 clipping is partial; Q25 loading delivered (F13c); Q26 scrollbars is partial (F14 delivered, F14b next after typography); Q35 typography is the next packet (F16); Q28 exhaustive components is starting with Button (F15.1); Q28's message is cut off after "We need to". The size framework (Q28, Q21) is written from the owner's words in [Button](#button-f151) for confirmation; no other size message exists in the logs.

| Q65 | 11-08 20:34 | "Develop and entire framework and philosophy around transitions and animations. What I want is a functional transition that conveys how context and content move from one state to the next. I want to rely heavily on morphing of content and content seamlessly and continuously wherever it makes sense. The idea is that if a context/content is present in both state, whether the same or an evolved one, the content should ideally morph seamlessly instead of disappearing and reappearing again. This can become a complex and expensive operation when done and applied everywhere. So, we ca be smart and optimal about it. Many state transition can only transition the wrapper and fake the content within. Various transition of a dialog into a success/error msg etc can all morph into smotth seamless transition by using the same wrapper so that nothing is flickering and breaks seamlessness. Any addition of shadoes, transition of border color, bg color etc should all be animated in value rather than a sudden jump and change in value. We don't want this at the cost of making the experience feel laggy. So we do most of these at 200ms time unless specifically extended where needed and in special places, we might want to completely not have this transition, like while changing the numeric value of an number input using arrow key, any lag in reflecting the value will give a laggy fill. We have to clearly identify wherre 0 latency is expected and not have any transition in such places. Use Opus 5.5 extra high thinking and detailed of this framework first. Critique it and improve it. Use the impreved version to audit and prepare a report of the recommended improvements and implementation in the design library. Implement these recommendations using opus 5.5 high." | **Q65.1 Framework** (Foundation, "Motion and transitions"; Opus 5.5 extra-high): a philosophy of functional transitions (they show how context and content move between states); morph-first continuity (content present in both states, same or evolved, morphs instead of disappearing and reappearing); a cost model with cheap strategies (animate the wrapper and cross-fade or fake the content within; shared-element morphs only where they pay off); same-wrapper morphs for state changes (dialog → success/error, button → progress → done, and so on) with no flicker; every visual value change (shadow, border colour, background, radius, size) interpolated, never jumped; a 200 ms default with named, justified longer durations; an explicit **zero-latency list** (direct manipulation and value entry: typing, NumberField arrows/drag, slider drag, scrubbing, caret, selection, hover-tracked pointers) with no transition; reduced motion; interruption/reversal; performance rules (compositor-friendly properties, layout-animation budget); tokens for durations and easings. **Q65.2 Critique and improve** the first draft (written critique and the changes it drove). **Q65.3 Audit report** of the library against the improved framework: every component and layout, findings with severity and a recommended fix and implementation plan. **Q65.4 Implement** the recommendations (Opus 5.5 high): motion tokens, shared primitives (value transitions, wrapper morph, shared-element), zero-latency places verified, demos on the library pages | [Motion and transitions](motion.md) (framework, critique, audit); ### Template for a component's DoD

Copy for each component packet (F15.x). One row per observable result; "Try it" names the page and story.

| ID | Done when (observable) | Evidence / Try it | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C.1 Sizes | Every size the coverage table marks must, side by side | Matrix page | |
| C.2 Types | Every must type, from one component where possible | Matrix page | |
| C.3 States | Rest, hover, pressed, focus-visible, disabled, unavailable with reason, plus the component's own (loading, selected, invalid…) | Matrix page | |
| C.4 Contexts | Light, dark and more contrast; the four space and four shape options | Title-bar controls, space/shape preview | |
| C.5 Accessibility | Name, role, keys, 44 px target or written exception, contrast | Accessibility section of the page | |
| C.6 Owner rows | Every Q row that applies, checked | This table | |

### Button (F15.1)

Size framework, written from Q28 and Q21 for the owner to confirm: size is **per instance** in seven steps (`xs` 24, `s` 36, `normal` 44, `m` 48, `l` 56, `xl` 64, `xxl` 80 px high), each a token set (height, padding, gap, icon, type); the theme's **space option** changes the space around and between components, never a component's size; the **shape option** changes radii at every size; `normal` and up keep at least a 44 px target, `xs` and `s` are dense sizes under the written exception (WCAG 2.5.8 minimum 24 px).

| ID | Done when (observable) | Evidence / Try it | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B.1 | Seven sizes, xs to xxl, side by side for text, icon + text and icon-only | [Button](http://127.0.0.1:8766/#/button) "Sizes and types" | Done (F15.1) |
| B.2 | Types: icon button (named, with its name as tooltip), dropdown button (MenuButton at the same sizes), segmented (SegmentedControl, no second component), button group (fundamental) | [ButtonGroup](http://127.0.0.1:8766/#/buttongroup), MenuButton in the size matrix, Button "Segmented button" | Done (F15.1b, about 21:14): split button = Button + icon-only MenuButton in an attached ButtonGroup (no new component); MenuButton gains emphasis, icon-only and busy (spinner inside, same width, won't open); SegmentedControl on the seven-step scale (`s` default, old `sm`/`md` = `xs`/`s`). Open: the ring variant for MenuButton |
| B.3 | In progress for every type, two variants: spinner inside, and an arc travelling around the button's border; no width change; busy announced; arc ≥ 3:1 | Button "In progress: spinner inside", "…on the border", "Try both" | Done (F15.1) |
| B.4 | Danger emphasis; unavailable with a reason (focusable, says why) | Button "Emphasis", "Unavailable" | Done (F15.1) |
| B.5 | Every size under the four space and four shape options keeps its height; only radii and surrounding space change | Space and shape pages' whole-library preview, then the Button page | Done by construction (size tokens alias size.*, never space.*); not yet inspected in all eight options |
| B.6 | Q34 holds: no one-sided borders, no redundant variant, Switch still the binary setting | Review | Self-checked; toggle buttons not built (owner decision open) |

| F.7 Differences from the move | (1) The error line takes Field's icon on TextField, NumberField and Select (TextArea already had it), so a long message can wrap one line sooner. (2) The box's error mark now shows only for a failed save, so a validation error has one mark (the message's), not two. (3) Field's messages no longer keep an empty row's gap after a hint or count: every Field with a hint is 4 px shorter (the four inputs were never affected). (4) Unavailable inputs still keep a full-colour label (Field dims it); unifying that is a visible change left for review | TextField "States", Select "States", NumberField "Mixed, limits and errors" | Done (F15.18), (4) open |
| F.8 Not yet | Horizontal groups; label info tip | — | Later |

## Future product Definition of Done

These rows are targets, not achieved results.

| ID | Promise / capability | Evidence needed | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PD1 | P1/U1: component reuse does not produce divergent behavior. | Source-derived component purpose/props/tokens/stories; representative flows; no redundant variants or tier violations. | Not implemented |
| PD2 | P1/U1/U2: accessibility works across themes, states and viewports. | Keyboard and screen-reader observations, accessibility checks, contrast measurements, 320 px layout and focus/scrolling checks. | Not implemented |
| PD3 | P1/U2: tokens and validated view data drive composition. | Deterministic generated token output, alias/ownership/contrast tests, data-validation failures and no view-specific style escape. | Not implemented |
| PD4 | P2/U3: decisions and quality claims remain grounded. | Linked assumptions and decisions, updated as-built notes, tests/observations beside each completed row. | Intent documented; product evidence absent |
| PD5 | P1/P2: waits, notifications and failures remain coherent. | Local pending/confirmed/failed state examples; input preserved, recovery available, auto-dismiss default verified, motion/announcements checked. | Not implemented |
| PD6 | P2: performance claims are measurable. | Representative workload, chosen metric/budget, reproducible baseline and before/after comparison. | Not measured |
| PD7 | P3/U4/U7: customization spans declared web UI families without replacing fundamentals. | Proposed first matrix: three UI families, two distinct brands, light/dark/more-contrast; zero component forks or per-view style escapes, and unchanged control behavior. Coverage gaps explicitly recorded. | Proposed target; not implemented |
| PD8 | P4/U5/U6: agents can make reliable contract-driven changes. | Proposed first corpus: six declared tasks (composition and theme adaptation in each of three UI families), all yielding schema-valid proposals within at most one repair round; baseline comparison of time and correction effort in a fixed environment before any "easiest" claim. | Proposed target; no agent baseline |
| PD9 | P2/P4/U6: invalid customization is recoverable and explainable. | All invalid fixture proposals rejected before replacing working output; machine-readable path/reason/recovery, affected-consumer diff and last working configuration preserved. | Proposed target; not implemented |
| PD10 | P1/P3/U8: add-ons are isolated and removable. | Enable on one/selected/all views and disable per subtree/theme; zero fundamental forks/private selectors, unchanged content/actions/focus in unaffected scopes, clean mount/update/dispose. Core works with zero effect-engine/asset requests when disabled. | Proposed; not implemented |
| PD11 | P1/P3/U9: font family/provider swaps need no component edits. | Change only validated theme/provider data; inspect required scripts/weights, text wrapping, blocked/offline/system fallback and metrics. Initial font transfer at most 100 KB compressed for declared script/weights, font CLS at most 0.02 and total CLS at most 0.1 on reference profiles; additional scripts have explicit budgets. | Proposed; no font implementation or measurements |
| PD12 | P1/P3/U10: at least three icon sets retain semantic coverage. | First-flow inventory mapped in Material Symbols, Fluent and Font Awesome Free; 100% names have approved meaning/size/style/RTL mapping or explicit acceptance-blocking gaps with reviewed fallbacks. Same ONE Icon fundamental; initial used-icon data at most 20 KB compressed, zero external icon requests or unreferenced geometry. | Proposed; no provider mappings built |
| PD13 | P2/U11: licence/provenance manifest is complete. | 100% selected fonts/icons/add-on assets have immutable origin/version, licence/use-rights, copyright/NOTICE/credit and redistribution/modification/brand restrictions; generated/distributed output retains applicable notices. No forbidden FFL/Remix packaging or kube.io assets; effect release gates resolved before distribution. | Proposed; research caveats documented, no distribution |
| PD14 | P1/P2/P3/U8: effects retain contrast and performance. | Effects on/off in light/dark/contrast, reduced transparency/motion, forced colours, unsupported browser/WebGL/context loss: every text role at least 4.5:1 and non-text/focus at least 3:1 on worst-case composited surfaces. Proposed p75 LCP at most 2.5 s, INP at most 200 ms, total CLS at most 0.1; at 60 Hz, p95 scrolling frame time at most 16.7 ms and no idle/offscreen animation loop. Same-machine effects-on/off comparison with noise allowance; otherwise limit scope or disable. | Proposed; no effect/performance baseline |

These initial thresholds are reviewable working defaults, not measurements or proof of absolute universality. The owner can revise the matrix/corpus before implementation. No project design-system or agent benchmark tests have been run on this draft.

| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| PD16 | P1/P3/U12/U13: specimens are faithful across contexts. | `specimen-renderer-fidelity`, `specimen-context-matrix`, `specimen-host-accessibility`: actual product renderer/token/story digests, no docs overrides, all declared context cells explicit, keyboard/portals and first product-host comparison verified. | Proposed; no specimen runner |
| PD17 | P2/U13: a broken candidate cannot remove the approved diagnostic reference. | `frame-specimen-isolation`: inject candidate token/import/render/theme/protocol errors; approved frame/reference remain usable with path/reason/recovery. Plain HTML stays readable when both renderer asset sets are blocked. A browser-wide hang requires external recovery, not an asserted iframe guarantee. | Proposed; no failure-injection evidence |
| PD18 | P2/U3/U13: known-good promotion is explicit and recoverable. | `known-good-promotion-and-recovery`: exact artifact digest plus dated owner approval/context scope; mixed/stale/unapproved artifact rejected; atomic pointer switch and prior artifact retained; first tiny consumer's seed approval recorded. | Proposed; no known-good product artifact |

These are named future checks, not implemented files or tests run on unapproved designs. [Architecture and bootstrap](brief.md#the-library-presents-itself) define the ordinary consumer, stable frame and emergency diagnostic exception.

| ID | Observable result | Evidence | Status |

| F11.1 | Every page opens at its top, and the old page fades out completely before the new one fades in, within 200 ms of motion (owner F11, O52). | Measured in the dev server: about 120 ms to fully out, then render, then about 90 ms in; never two pages at once; focus on the new title; reduced motion swaps at once. | Built; owner judgement pending |
| F12.1 | A theme picks its space (compact, normal, loose, extra loose) and shape (sharp, rounded, soft, super soft) in one config, and the defaults reproduce today exactly (owner F12, O53-O55). | `npm run tokens:check` runs `tools/space-shape.mjs --check` (config, staleness, every invariant in all eight options); 1,140 of 1,140 resolved values unchanged at normal + rounded. | Built; owner judgement pending |
| F12.2 | A person can compare the four options of each side by side, read who each is for, and preview the whole library in any of them (U4, U12). | Space and size and Shape pages at `http://127.0.0.1:8766/#/space` and `#/shape`; 44 px targets unchanged and no sideways scroll at about 320 px in extra loose + super soft (browser check). | Built; owner judgement pending |
| F13a.1 | Anyone can tell where a new floating thing goes, and nothing stacks by a raw number (owner F13, O56). | `npm run check:layers` passes (10 layers, 6 uses, 0 raw z-index); the Layers page lists each layer with its components, read from source. | Built; owner judgement pending |

**Against the promise:** Stage 0 and this draft make the project ready to reason, execute and document. P1/P3/P4 are not yet fulfilled as a usable adaptable component library or an AI-friendly framework. Core proof comes first; source extraction, provider swapping/coverage, add-on isolation/removal, licence completeness and effects-on contrast/performance then need their proposed evidence. Source review is not integration validation or licence clearance: UI Lighting's scoped fit, Liquid Glass's release licence/asset gate, restricted font/icon distribution and all budgets remain future checks. Component implementation waits for a separately approved bounded stage.

Self-presentation and its known-good recovery are now specified, not built. Rapid review is the current operating agreement, with two distinct documentation deliveries; product approval and the longer three-round outcome remain unproven. No frame promotion or execution-mode escalation follows from these documentation checks.

## Stage: DesignUp improvements

> **TL;DR:** The Definition of Done for the work that comes out of the first real adoption (owner Q104, Q105, Q107, Q108): six promises, twelve things a person can do, and observable rows per release (0.2.0-beta.2, 0.3.0-beta.1, 0.4.0-beta.1 "Data-driven sites", later) plus rows for the stage as a whole. The plan behind it is the [improvement plan](improvement-plan.md). The coverage matrix below maps every item in both DesignUp notes, the plan's own findings and the Beta known issues to a row here or to a recorded out-of-scope decision, with **0 unmapped**. The stage's final evidence is DesignUp's own re-run (BS.8). **Q110 adds** promise DUP7, user-can U13–U14, [Migration readiness](#migration-readiness-q110) rows BM.1–BM.6 (0.4.0-beta.2) and B5.1–B5.8 (0.5.0-beta.1), stage rows BS.9–BS.10, and a second matrix for DesignUp's migration-readiness note: 120 items, 100 to rows, 20 out of scope with reasons, **0 unmapped**. **Q111 re-checks** the note's final form: rows BM.7–BM.8 and B5.9, and the matrix grows to 129 items, 108 to rows, 21 out of scope, **0 unmapped**.

**Owner, 2026-11-10 02:08 (verbatim, [Q108](#owner-instructions)):** *"Create a proper DoD and make sure everything is covered from the notes from the DesignUp site."*

**Sources:** the DesignUp beta report (`docs/research/design-library-beta-report.md` in that project) and its data-driven requirement (`docs/research/design-library-data-driven-requirement.md`), both read-only; [Improvement plan](improvement-plan.md) Parts 1–3; [Handoff readiness](handoff-readiness.md#beta-known-issues). Results are recorded beside each row by whoever does the work; the "State" column starts at Planned, and rows already built say so.

### Promises

| # | Promise | Done when | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DUP1 | A brand-led site stays inside the library: its brand needs are met through public seams, not custom parts that redraw core | DesignUp's expressive pages reach a library share of at least half on the re-run, with no core-token override outside the allow-list | BS.8; the project check's `--share` and `core-token` counts |
| DUP2 | Defaults are safe for any brand: text passes AA and motion stops when the person asks | The bright-brand set passes with the guard on; no page motion under reduce | B3.7, B2.3, B4.10 |
| DUP3 | A project enforces the rules itself, by running one command | `npx design-library-check` runs in a project and its findings match a hand audit | B3.20, B3.21, B4.12 |
| DUP4 | An agent gets it right from the guide: names that exist, core parts first | The names check passes; the next proof's share and workaround counts drop | B2.2, B3.27, B3.1, B3.2, BS.5 |
| DUP5 | A site can be strictly data-driven (D1–D8) | AT1–AT6 pass on the reference site | B4.14 |
| DUP6 | Upgrading never breaks a site, and each release says how to adopt it | The upgrade proof on a DesignUp copy shows no change; each release ships a brief | BS.2, BS.3, BS.4 |
| DUP7 | A site moves onto the library completely, keeping only its brand art as named template parts on public extension points (Q110) | DesignUp reports 43 or more of its 51 custom items on core or tokens and the rest inside its six named parts, with no other custom component code | BS.9; BM.1–BM.6, B5.1–B5.8 |
| DUP8 | Pictures and illustrations belong to every scheme: a site's art looks right in light, dark and more contrast, page-wide or in one region, and drawn art fits its space and moves only as the motion rules allow (Q112) | The context-matrix stories of `Image` and `Illustration` pass with no flash or shift on a switch; `check:svg` and the content check pass on every template | BM.9–BM.11, B5.10–B5.14 |

### What a person can do

| # | A person can |
| --- | --- |
| U1 | Install both packages on the npm the guide names, behind a package proxy, first time |
| U2 | Set the page frame, link look, canvas colour, tracking, fonts, header, footer and scheme from the theme, without overriding core |
| U3 | Build common site needs (link buttons, tinted bands, uneven columns, media openings, lead text, crops and treatments) from core props and slots |
| U4 | Pick any brand colour and get AA text |
| U5 | Turn on reduced motion and have every page change, scroll and effect respect it, with no site code |
| U6 | Search a list as they type, and send a form whose fields save together |
| U7 | Follow in-page links, "on this page" lists and letter jumps under hash or history routing |
| U8 | Run one command that lists every rule break with its file, line and fix |
| U9 | Find the core part for a need before writing a custom one |
| U10 | Start an empty template of their own |
| U11 | Add a page, reorder sections, change words or add a collection item by editing JSON alone |
| U12 | Upgrade by following one brief, with nothing breaking |
| U13 | Move between sibling pages (years, versions, items of one kind) without losing their place, and go back to the top of the page they're on from its link |
| U14 | Put brand shapes over or around a core picture, reveal a paired picture in the site's own way, and keep a series of openings the same height, without a custom frame or opening component |
| U15 | Give any picture, photo or drawing, a light and a dark (and a more-contrast) version, in code or JSON, that switches with the scheme of the page or of one region, with nothing flashing or jumping (Q112) |
| U16 | Place a drawn illustration that fits whatever space it's in, takes its colours from the theme in every scheme, and moves only as the motion rules allow, still under reduced motion (Q112) |
| U17 | Use their brand's own icons: a whole set, or just a few that replace the same icons everywhere components and layouts show them, the rest staying as they were (Q113) |

| BS.2 | Non-breaking (Q105.3): every change is an addition, opt-in, or a deprecation with its alias; the only default change is IP3's, under reduced motion only; new checks warn | Changelog has no "Breaking" heading for 0.2.0-beta.2, 0.3.0-beta.1 or 0.4.0-beta.1; the upgrade proof (BS.4) | Read each release's changelog | Q105.3 | Planned |
| BS.3 | Each release ships a copy-paste adoption brief for an agent: install line, "nothing breaks" with the proof, what's new, optional steps each naming the workaround it replaces and its file, what to watch, how to verify, and the data-driven steps (Q105.4) | `packages/core/UPGRADE.md` and `docs/adopt-{version}.md` registered in the docs manifest | Paste the brief into an agent in a copy of a site | Q105.4 | Planned |
| BS.4 | Before each release, a copy of DesignUp outside both repositories installs the new tarballs and type-checks, builds and renders every route with no new console errors and no visual change against the same copy on the previous version; the copy is deleted after (Q105.5) | Proof record in Handoff readiness: install, `tsc`, build, per-route screenshots or DOM metrics compared, console logs | Read the proof record for the release | Q105.5 | Planned |

### Against the promise

Built so far: B2.1–B2.7 (install matrix and guide fixes; reduced-motion swap, theme settings, link decoration, base colour, tracking). Everything else is planned. The promise is furthest away on DUP5 (data-driven sites: nothing of 0.4 is built) and on DUP1, which only DesignUp's re-run can prove (BS.8). The riskiest assumptions to settle first are IA13 (one page schema for templates and sites) and IA9 (shared breakpoints in CSS); both have a cheap test in the plan. Every row that depends on DesignUp's team (BS.8) names them: we never write in that project.
